Now I need to plan the C1 offline demo second-run. Key design: after the first session runs and revisions are accepted, run a second session with the revised resume text and parentSessionId. The scripted demo must produce different verdicts for the second run — e.g. C2 (overstated) becomes solid after the fix.

The scripted demo's `respond` is driven by prompts. In the second run, the resume text is the revised text. The claims extracted will be different (the revised text). So the scripted demo needs to handle: extract from a revised resume → produce claims that anchor to the revised text. Then probes/verdicts/suggestions for those.

Now I understand scripted-demo fully. To make a second run (改完再跑一遍) in offline demo, I need a second scripted client variant that produces different verdicts for the revised resume.

Design decision. The demo seed currently runs 9 combos of (difficulty × style), each a single session. Adding a second session per combo would double cassette count and complicate. Better approach: add a **separate, dedicated** demo second-run in the default combo only? Hmm, but that would produce cassettes that only work when default combo is selected. Actually cassettes are keyed by prompt digest; second run only happens when user clicks 「改完再跑一遍」 which is available in any combo. If offline demo only has second-run cassettes for the default combo, clicking rerun in other combos would fail with cassette-missing.

Hmm. That's a real constraint. Options:
1. Add second run for all 9 combos → ~ doubles cassette count (56 → ~100+).
2. Make the second-run script deterministic in a way that reuses the same prompts. But revised resume text differs → extract prompt differs → new digest necessarily. So no way to avoid new cassettes for each combo.

Let's check: probe prompt includes contracts (difficulty affects budget section) and resume. answer prompt includes resume text + question. verdict depends on claim + transcript (contract? maybe). suggest depends on style.

So a second run per combo: extract(1 shared) + jd-extract/jd-merge/jd-match (depend on JD text + claims; jd-match prompt includes claims → claims texts differ after revision → maybe new) + probe(per difficulty, 3 × maybe) + answer(shared if questions same) + verdict(shared if transcript changed...) etc.

OK let me design a helper:

```ts
/** 这一场这条主张的原文引文是哪一句：改过之后引文就变成改后的写法 */
function currentQuote(plan: DemoClaimPlan, prompt: string): string | null {
  if (prompt.indexOf(plan.quote) >= 0) return plan.quote;
  const s = plan.suggestion;
  if (s === null) return null;
  const candidates = [s.after, ...Object.values(s.afterByStyle)];
  for (const c of candidates) if (c !== undefined && prompt.indexOf(c) >= 0) return c;
  return null;
}
```

And `verdictOf(plan, quote)`: quote === plan.quote ? plan.verdict : plan.revisedVerdict.

The offline demo second run is complex. Let me reconsider: is it necessary? The offline demo (replay mode) is what judges see without an API key. If C1's "改完再跑一遍" can't be demonstrated offline, the demo video can't show it. That's important — the competition's key deliverable is the demo.

How does the offline demo work? `npm run seed:demo` writes cassettes into fixtures/llm keyed by prompt fingerprint. The replay client only knows purposes and prompts. A second session with a rewritten resume would produce different prompts → different fingerprints → missing cassettes. So for offline replay to support the second run, we need cassettes for those prompts too.

Approach: seed a second session in demo-seed that: (a) creates a first session, (b) accepts revisions for C2, (c) creates a second session with parentSessionId and the revised resume text, (d) runs it. The scripted client must produce responses for the rewritten resume's extract prompt — which yields claims extracted from the rewritten resume text. Hmm, that's a problem: the extract script returns a fixed DEMO_PLANS-based list of 5 claims whose quotes come from the ORIGINAL resume. After rewriting, e.g. C2's quote line changes to "引入 Redis 缓存后，同一接口在预发环境单机压测下 QPS 由 800 提升到 5000（压测工具与口径见附录）" — so the quote no longer exists verbatim. The extract for the revised resume would produce claims that fail anchoring and be dropped.

So the scripted client needs to be "resume-aware": extract should return claims anchored to the actual resume text in the prompt. That's a significant redesign.

Simpler alternative: make the scripted demo's second run use a scripted extraction that reads the resume text from the prompt and emits claims by matching lines of the revised resume. Since the scripted client gets the full prompt (which includes the resume outline), it can look for the revised C2 paragraph text and emit a claim with that quote. Then the probe/answer/verdict/suggest scripts key off claimId → but claimIds get renumbered. Hmm.

Alternative much simpler approach: Keep DEMO_PLANS as the script, but make the script match claims by **quote prefix / keyword** rather than by claimId index. Then for the second run, extraction emits claims whose quotes are found in the prompt's resume text: for each DEMO_PLAN, if the original quote is present verbatim → use it; else if a "revisedQuote" is present → use that (and remember "this claim is now the revised version, so verdict is solid"). That's a targeted approach: add to DemoClaimPlan an optional `afterText`/`afterQuote` + `afterVerdict` describing the post-rewrite state.

That's doable but touches the scripted demo's matching logic (planForClaimId used widely). Risk of breaking 5 cassette tests + 9 combos × cassettes.
